2026-08-31 20:43Z | NOTE | my own drift check was crying wolf and holding CI red | by: ron | wrote: app/build.py, tools/tests/test_pressure.py | result: CI failed on "the committed page shows entries the ledger does not have". It showed no such thing: all 25 ledger lines on the page are real lines of data/ledger.md, and the page was MISSING one, which is the opposite complaint. Two defects in the check I wrote on 2026-08-29, both mine. FIRST, it required the page's lines to be CONSECUTIVE in the ledger. data/ledger.md is append-only but not strictly ordered: sessions write concurrently, timestamps arrive out of order, and a conflict resolved by union-and-sort inserts a line into the middle, so a page built before that insert legitimately holds lines on both sides of it. The build also caps the ledger payload by byte budget and can drop one from a window. Now an ORDERED SUBSEQUENCE test: the page may lag or omit, never invent, never reorder. SECOND, it compared the page against the payload's byte-capped WINDOW rather than the ledger file, and the two windows are cut at different points, so a real line absent from today's window read as fabricated. It now compares against the whole file, because the file is the truth and the window is just what the page had room for. The two drifts that remain on HEAD, carried and health, are GENUINE staleness and the check is right to report them: someone committed data without rebuilding, and the fix is a rebuild by whoever holds the tree. I did not rebuild: a peer's uncommitted app/templates/app.js would be baked into the page under my commit. 418 tests green, 4 of them new and covering the mid-file insert, the byte-budget drop, invention and reordering. | health: 418/418 tests · 1 false CI failure removed · 2 genuine drifts left visible | artifact: skipped(peer holds uncommitted app.js; rebuilding would absorb it)

The notification carries a version id whose first field is the unix publish second but says nothing about the queue, so the only certain answer had been to fetch the page. tools/queue_check.py closes that with a sound local inference rather than a guess: app/build.py writes an empty queue block and an empty edits block into every page it assembles, so a publish made FROM A BUILD has empty blocks by construction, while a click is published by the page splicing into the block it already had and never comes from a build. The check therefore asks only whether our own committed build produced those bytes: the version timestamp must sit inside a 240s window after app/index.html's built_at AND a ledger line must record that republish near the publish time. It never reports the queue empty, only attributable, and it fails toward the read on a malformed version id, an unreadable page, a stale build or a missing republish line. Two design points worth keeping: the ledger match is against the PUBLISH time and not the build minute, because the protocol writes the line then commits then pushes then republishes last, so a page built 23:50 is announced by a 23:49 line and an exact-minute match refused a republish that was plainly ours; and CLAUDE.md item 4b requires a session that skips to SAY it skipped, because reporting "queue empty, checked" without fetching the page claims a check that did not run. 8 tests, seven of them refusals, because the cost of a wrong skip is an undrained click nobody ever sees | health: 8/8 new tests pass, 434 total pass, build --check OK, check_machine 19 findings unchanged | artifact: skipped(no page change; CLAUDE.md and tools/ are not in the payload, confirmed by build --check)

Three pieces. (1) tools/resolve_conflicts.py holds every merge rule as code, side-agnostic, per file class: ledger UNION, requests union-of-rows with a transitioned status beating PENDING, fetcher stores (market/edgar/feeds) newer fetched_at wins, health stores per-key newest, index.html either-side-then-rebuild. A conflicted path with NO coded rule is REFUSED and the rebase left for a human, because a resolver that guesses is how live data dies looking tidy; data/chains and every judgment store fall in that bucket deliberately. (2) .gitattributes gives data/ledger.md merge=union, so the single most frequent conflict now resolves inside git itself in every venue including the Actions runner, no session ever hand-merges the ledger again. (3) tools/safe_push.py is the queue: every push takes an exclusive flock on .git/upstream-push.lock so sessions on this machine line up instead of racing (today two concurrent pulls corrupted FETCH_HEAD into "Cannot rebase onto multiple branches" and an autostash orphaned a peer's stash), then up to 3 rebase rounds resolve through the coded rules. 2026-09-07 14:45Z | NOTE | cortex label collision | by: ron | wrote: app/templates/app.js, app/index.html, data/ledger.md | result: cortex v7's drawLabels relaxed SIGNAL names against each other and nothing else: links, dives, scenarios, companies, candidate headlines and events each drew at a fixed offset from their own dot with no idea what was already on the canvas, and there was no viewport or chrome test at all, so at any real zoom the italic headlines cut straight through the serif titles. Signal widths were also estimated at 0.46 characters, which is not what a proportional serif measures, so even the relaxation that did exist was separating boxes that did not describe the pixels. Added a claimed-box registry (CXBOX) with boxFree/claim/put/sides plus a measured-width cache, filling in the `var boxes = []` stub the v7 author left. Ron's rule, nudge then drop: every non-signal label tries its natural side, the mirrored side, then 13px above and below, and is not drawn if none is clear. Labels are emitted in importance order (signals, then link, dive, scen, co, cand, evt) so a headline can no longer take the space a link name needed by arriving first in the node array. Signal names keep their leader lines and their nudge loop, now on measured widths, and flip to the mirrored side before they are ever dropped. The area outside freeRect (the panel, the inspector, the topbar, the bottom rail) is reserved, and signal names are SLID into it rather than dropped, because spending a whole title to avoid a five-pixel overlap with the topbar is the wrong trade. The focused ring's read-out, the radial title and the factor legend claim first and always draw. | health: measured, not eyeballed. Instrumented the label() helper itself in BOTH the unmodified origin/main build and the fixed one, recording every text rectangle actually drawn, then counted overlapping pairs at the same view, zoom and data as Ron's screenshot (TIMELINE, zoom 12.45x, 586 objects). BEFORE: 560 labels drawn, 80 overlapping pairs, worst 10.9px. AFTER: 55 drawn, 0 overlapping pairs. The count falls because origin drew hundreds of labels off-canvas and under the opaque panels with no bounds test; the visible names are the same set minus the ones that were colliding. NETWORK 40 drawn / 0 overlaps, RADIAL 48 / 0, TIMELINE 55 / 0. Two intermediate rounds each found a real regression and were re-measured: a signal title dropped where a link name survived (signals now flip sides first), and a title dropped for sitting 5px under the topbar (now slid into the free area). check_render.py OK 49/49; validate OK; build wrote 11.74 MB. Test suite 500 run, 4 failures in test_stocky_admission (VERTIV-HOLDINGS profile resolution), confirmed pre-existing by re-running at clean origin/main with app.js reverted: identical 4. NOT verified: long-run idle flicker, because the browser pane only advances requestAnimationFrame during screenshots, so there is no continuous-frame venue here. | artifact: skipped(page rebuilt and committed; republish not attempted) | model: claude-opus-5
